Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

Analyst Industry Views

below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: We factor in WNC 2Q26 result and revise up earnings by 1%/ 2%/ 2% 
in 2026-28E to reflect higher than expected revenues of WiFi 7/ WiFi 8 and switch 
products, driven by spec upgrade and rising client demand. We keep GM and Opex ratio 
largely unchanged.

Valuation: With the earning estimate changes, we raise our 12M TP to NT$380.0 (vs. 
previously at NT$371, based on 28.0x 2027E P/E, unchanged). We continue to base 
our TP on 2027E P/E, and the target multiple is still derived from the correlation 
between P/E and EPS growth of its peers, which is higher as we mark to market. Our 
target P/E of 28.0x is higher than company’s avg. +1 std P/E (16.3x), which we believe is 
not stretched considering the sustainable growth of WNC driven by long-term satellite 
and high speed networking demand. Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology: WNC 
Valuation: We use a near-term P/E to derive our 12m target price for WNC, consistent 
with our Taiwan Technology coverage. We base our TP of NT$380.0 on a target P/E 
multiple of 28.0x on a forward year EPS (2027E). Our target P/E is derived from the 
correlation between P/E and EPS growth of its peers. We are Buy rated on WNC. Key 
downside risks: fiercer-than-expected competition in satellite communication, 
slower-than-expected Wi-Fi 7 or 5G FWA ramp-up in US and Europe markets.

耕興 (6146 TT)  Buy

耕興評價已修正，有機會受惠轉單效應，給 Buy 的投資建
議。  
投資建議 ：美國 FCC 有機率禁止，銷往美國的電子產品在中國
實驗室進行測試，耕興有望接受大量轉單，給 Buy 的投資建議。  
公司簡介： 耕興主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規
檢測服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零
組件之銷售，全球知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、
網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一： FCC 公布
1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機 ： 路透社 (Reuters) 報導 ，
FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電子產品，
是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試 ， 若表決通過 ，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利： 耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，
若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室 ， 將會有大量相關訂單轉移至台灣廠商 ，
耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

2 群益投顧 
  ESG ：  
Environmental ：與 2021 年相比， 2022 年之用電度數較 2021 年減少 0.68% 、
減紙 42%( 減碳 7.92 公噸 CO 2 )。未來規劃：華亞實驗室建置太陽能發電系
統；建置雨水或空調冷凝水回收系統；分階段換發 LED 節能燈具、省電空
調；並於馬達設備增設變頻器以節省用電。  
Social ：體恤員工與資助社團法人嘉義縣扶緣服務協會、新港文教基金會、
與新港奉天宮之金虎爺慢跑活動。國內交易量一百萬元以上的供應商中，
92% 已簽屬「供應商社會責任承諾書」並定期查核；零件部跟生產相關的原
料供應商皆已簽署「不使用衝突礦產聲明書」。  
Governance ：董事會治理制度包含「董事會議事規範」、衡量績效目標落實
之「董事績效評估辦法」、確保誠信經營之「誠信經營守則」、避免貪腐與
符合公平交易法之「誠信經營作業程序及行為指南」等。訂定「資訊安全政
策規範」並以 PDCA (Plan, Do, Check, Act) 循環方式管理。  
公司簡介：  
耕興成立於 1997 年，主要從事電子產品之電磁相容 (EMC) 認證、安規檢測
服務、無線網通認證、手機認證、數位電視認證及防磁零組件之銷售，全球
知名的手機、平板電腦與 NB 業者、晶片廠、網通廠等多為耕興的客戶。  
由於耕興具備 GCF 、 PTCRB 組織的符合性檢測認證，以及加拿大與日本發
證權，只要客戶通過公司認證後即可銷往多國，可降低檢驗所需時間與成
本。  
根據 2022 年 FCC 所公布的資料，耕興在 5G/Wi-Fi 的檢測市占率已經是世
界第一，產能也是全球第一。手機 /網通賣的款式越多，對耕興越有利，單
一產品的出貨量對耕興營收無關。  
台灣無線通訊檢測相關同業包含東研信超 (6840 TT) 、穩得 (6761 TT) 和敦吉
(2459 TT) ，耕興的策略是不斷地去投入比同業多的 CAPEX ，做最先進規格
的檢測，像是毫米波、 Wi-Fi 7 的檢測，以提升營收與獲利。  
營收占比 _地區別：  
浩興為耕興的中國子公司。

資料來源：耕興法說簡報。

### Page 3

個股報告  
                                        2026 年 10 月 14 日

3 群益投顧 
AI 筆電、企業級 AP 檢測需求帶動耕興 1H26 營運成長：  
耕興表示， 1H26 獲利增長主要來自於實體 AI 終端裝置發展同時結合非
MRA(Mutual Recognition Arrangement ，相互承認協議 ) 國家地區 FCC 
PAG(Pre-Approval Guidance) 轉單效應帶動大量高階機種檢測需求。  
耕興內部統計最顯著增長的兩大產業族群來自於 AI 筆電及北美企業級 AP
檢測訂單需求增加。  
2H26 營運有望逐季成長：  
展望 2H26 同樣伴隨 PAG 轉單效應持續消化大量湧入的 AI 筆電及北美企業
級 AP 檢測訂單。檢測需求帶動下， 3Q26 獲利表現將有機會比 2Q26 強勁，
同時 4Q26 的獲利也有機會更上一層樓， 4Q26 是 2027 年高階旗艦級手機種
換機潮的檢測高峰期。  
耕興的 5G 產品、高階網通的檢測市佔率全球第一：  
FCC 公布 1H26 的 5G 產品、高階網通檢測的耕興市佔率，分別為 23.97% 、
47.3% ，皆為全球第一。  
FCC 將投票決定中國實驗室未來的商機：  
路透社 (Reuters) 報導， FCC 將於 10/29/2026 投票表決，來決定賣到美國的電
子產品，是否要禁止在中國實驗室 (非 MRA 國家 )進行測試，若表決通過，
預計將於 12/2028 開始生效。  
FCC 主席表示， 2025 年賣到美國的電子產品，約有 82% 是在中國實驗室進
行測試，而在美國實驗室測試的比例不到 4%。  
若 FCC 表決通過，對耕興有利：  
耕興具備 FCC 的 MRA 資格，若 FCC 禁止中國實驗室，將會有大量相關訂
單轉移至台灣廠商，耕興檢測產能為全球第一，將直接受惠轉單效應。

7 群益投顧 
  【投資評等說明】  
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首次評等潛在上漲空間 ≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy) 5% ≤首次評等潛在上漲空間 <15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無法由基本面給予投資評等  
預期近期股價將處於盤整  
建議降低持股

【投資評等調整機制】  
  投資評等係「首次」給予特定個股投資評等時，其前一日收盤價相較 3 個月
目標價之潛在上漲空間計算而得。個股投資評等分為四個等級，定義如上。
爾後的投資評等係依循「首次評等」，直到停止推薦。   
「強力買進」、「買進」及「區間操作」均有 upside 目標價。差別僅在於，
首次評等時潛在報酬率不同。「中立」則無目標價。  
一旦我們給予特定個股「強力買進」 、 「買進」或「區間操作」之投資評等，
就是責任的開始，爾後將透過各式研究報告作定期性、持續性追蹤基本面及
股價變化，直到停止推薦。  
停止推薦情境：  
1.  達目標價。  
2.  雖未達原訂目標價，但檢視基本面、訊息面、籌碼面等多方訊息，研
判股價上漲空間已然有限，將適時出具降評報告。  
3.  推薦後股價不漲反跌。

【免責聲明】  
  本研究報告僅提供予特定人之客戶作為參考資料「非經同意不得轉載」 。我
們並不確保此資訊的完整性與正確性，投資人應了解，報告中有關未來預測
之陳述可能不會實現，因而不應被依賴。而且此報告並非根據特定投資目的
或依預定對象之財務狀況所撰寫出來的，因此，此研究報告的目的，既非對
投資人於買賣證券、選擇權、期貨或其他證券相關之衍生性商品提供詢價服
務，亦非作為進行交易的要約。投資人應注意到相關證券之價值及收益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

SK하이닉스 솔리다임 상장, 성장과 주주가치를 잇는 조건

▶Analyst 박세연 shannon@hanwha.com 3772-7406

[주요 데이터 및 차트]

NPS 낸드 사업 반기 실적  
→ 순이익 0.13조원에서 5.84조원으로

주: NPS는 솔리다임의 모회사  
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

공모 구조별 SK하이닉스 몫 
→ 거래 직후 가치 같음(가정) 
구분 신주 100% 구주매출 100% 
SK하이닉스 몫 약 129조원 약 116조원 + 현금 13.3조원 
현금이 남는 곳 솔리다임 NPS → SK하이닉스 
전액 환원 가정 
주당 
공모 현금은 
솔리다임에 
약 1만 8,300원 
(종가의 1.3%)

주: 공모 전 지분가치 1,000억달러·조달 100억달러 가정 
자료: 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

솔리다임 상장 이 SK 하이닉스 주주에게 이익 이 될지 는 상장 여부가 아
니라 조 건이 정 합니 다. 보도 된 기업가치 1,000 억달 러(블 룸버그)는 약 
136조 원으 로 SK하이 닉 스 시 가총 액 의 13% 규모 입 니다. 확 인할 조건은 
세 가지, 상장 전 출자 가격 과 공모 자 금의 쓰임, 환원 계획 입니 다. 신
주라면 미국 투자 의 수익 을, 구주 매 출 이 라면 매각 대금 이 주주 에 게 돌
아오는 길을 봐야 합 니다. 판단 시점은 금 융위 원 회 유권 해 석과 10 월 
28 일 실적 발 표, 12월 3 일 재공 시 입 니 다.

다롄 생산기지, 미국 공장 검토 
미국은 다롄 공장(옛 인텔 다롄)을 장비 반입 포괄 허가 명단에서 빼고 
연간 허가로 전환(BIS, 2025). SK하이닉스 매출의 68.8%는 미국 판매
법인에서 발생. 솔리다임은 미국 동부에 낸드 공장 검토(Reuters, 
2026.9). 세계 데이터센터 전력 수요가 2030년 2.3배(2024년 대비)로 
늘 전망이라 대용량 SSD 수요가 커지는 것도 배경(IEA, 2025)

해외 상장에도 중복상장 규율, 이사회 의무가 관건 
금감원장은 금융위·금감원 모두 중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답변
(10.19 국정감사). 국내 증권신고서 대상 여부는 금융위 유권해석 대
기. 해외 상장이라 거래소 특례심사는 받지 않고 주주동의는 권고 사
항. SK㈜·SK이노베이션·SK텔레콤은 NPS 신주 인수에 총 11.1억달러를 
약정. 사업 경쟁력과 자금 조달의 필요성, 기존 주주가치에 미치는 영
향을 함께 살펴볼 필요[그림7·12]

자금의 쓰임이 관건, 환원 가정 시 주당 약 1만8,300원 
공모가가 공정하다는 가정 아래, 거래 직후 SK하이닉스의 지분가치와 
현금 합계는 신주·구주 모두 약 129조원으로 같고 현금이 남는 곳만 
다름. 구주매출 100억달러 중 NPS를 거쳐 SK하이닉스에 귀속되는 현
금을 전액 배당하면 주당 약 1만8,300원. 10월 13일 종가의 1.3%, 
2025 사업연도 주당 배당의 약 6배(회사의 배당 계획이 아닌 가정 계
산). 신주 발행으로 조달한 자금을 투자하면 주주가치는 투자수익에 달
림[표4].

### Page 2

ESG [한화리서치]

2 
 I. 시장이 묻는 세 가지

1. 세 가지 질문과 확인 결과

솔리다임 상장, 
시장의 세 가지 질문 
 솔리다임 상장 보도(Bloomberg, 10/7) 뒤 시선은 SK하이닉스 주주가치로 향했다. 시장
의 질문은 세 가지다. 왜 미국 상장인가(II·III장), 상장 전 지분은 누구에게 가는가(IV
장), 주주에게 돌아올 몫은 얼마인가(V장). 
당국은 중복상장 
규율 적용 입장 
금감원장 · 10.19 국정감사 
 금감원장은 10월 19일 국정감사에서 금융위원회와 금융감독원 모두 솔리다임 상장을 
중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답했다. 다만 해외 상장이라 거래소 특례심사는 받지 않
고, 주주동의가 필수인 물적분할 자회사도 아니다. 표4 가정(추가 프리IPO 제외)대로면 
SK하이닉스의 솔리다임 실질 지분은 약 85~86%다(6월 말 95.08%). 국내 증권신고서 
대상인지는 금융위원회 유권해석을 기다리고 있다. 
미국 공시 체계, 
모회사는 이미 편입 
 미국 상장으로 엄격한 공시 규율을 따르면 기업가치를 높게 평가받을 수 있다는 것이 
자기구속(bonding) 가설이다(Coffee, 2002). 미국에 교차상장한 외국 기업의 토빈 q는 
같은 나라의 미국 미상장 기업보다 16.5% 높았다는 연구도 있다(Doidge 외, 2004, 
1997년 표본).  
Coffee, J. C., “Racing T owards the T op?: The Impact of Cross-Listings and Stock Market Competition on International Corporate 
Governance”, Columbia Law Review 102(7)

SK하이닉스는 7월 10일 나스닥 ADR 상장으로 이미 미국 공시 체계에 편입됐다. 솔리
다임 상장의 추가 효과는 자금 조달과 투자자 접근성 측면에서 따로 평가해야 한다. 
삼성전자와 차이 
1.2%p 이내 
 보도일과 다음 거래일 이틀 동안 SK하이닉스는 프리IPO 보도 때 5.2%, 주관사 선정 
때 6.8% 내렸다. 삼성전자(4.0%, 6.7%)보다 각각 1.2%p, 0.1%p 더 내렸다. 두 구간 모
두 SK하이닉스는 삼성전자와 비슷하게 움직였다(한 종목과 견준 이틀 관찰)[그림1]. 
10월 13일 낙폭은 
솔리다임 공시 없던 날 
 10월 13일 코스피는 5.46% 내린 6,035.35에 마감했고, SK하이닉스는 10.09%, 삼성전
자는 7.39% 내렸다. 이날 솔리다임 관련 공시는 없었다. SK하이닉스는 삼성전자보다 
2.7%p 더 내렸지만, 이 비교로는 그 원인을 가려낼 수 없다. 
[그림1] 솔리다임 보도 전후 이틀 주가 → 두 보도 구간 모두 삼성전자와 차이는 1.2%p 이내

3

2. 연구가 본 중복상장 할인의 조건

연구가 본 할인 요인, 
가치 이전 위험 
선행 연구 
 중복상장 할인의 근거로는 흔히 지배주주와 일반주주 사이의 이해상충이 꼽힌다. 대표
적인 예가 지배주주를 위해 회사의 자산과 이익을 옮기는 터널링이다(Johnson 외, 
2000). 지배권이 현금흐름권을 웃돌수록 기업가치는 낮았다(Claessens 외, 2002). 한국 
기업집단을 다룬 연구도 계열사 간 인수와 사모 발행이 소수주주 몫에 영향을 준다는 
점을 보여 줬다(Bae·Kang·Kim, 2002; Baek·Kang·Lee, 2006)[표1]. 그래서 시장은 계열
사 거래의 가격 산정 근거를 눈여겨본다. 
자금의 쓰임 따라 
모회사 반응도 달라 
 자회사 상장이 늘 악재는 아니다. 미국에서는 자회사 공모 발표 때 모회사 주가가 오르
는 경향이 있었다(Schipper·Smith, 1986). 공모 자금을 주로 부채 상환·배당에 쓰겠다고 
밝힌 표본의 누적초과수익률은 +6.63%, 투자 목적으로 보유하겠다고 밝힌 표본은 
−0.01%였다(Allen·McConnell, 1998). 할인 여부는 상장 자체보다 공모 구조와 자금의 
쓰임에 달려 있다. 
같은 상장도 
분위기 따라 달리 읽혀 
 같은 중복상장도 시장 분위기에 따라 다르게 읽힌다. 미국 기술주 거품기에는 자회사 
주식이 비싸게 거래돼, 모회사 시가총액이 보유 자회사 지분가치보다 낮아지는 가격 오
류까지 나타났다. 공매도가 어려워 이 오류는 한동안 풀리지 않았다(Lamont·Thaler, 
2003). 심리가 높았던 시기 뒤에는 가치를 매기기 어려운 주식의 수익률이 상대적으로 
낮았다(Baker·Wurgler, 2006). 솔리다임 보도일과 다음 거래일 이틀 동안 SK하이닉스 
주가는 8월과 10월 두 구간 모두 삼성전자와 비슷하게 움직였다[그림1].

[표1] 중복상장 관련 주요 연구 → 가치 이전 위험이 할인 요인으로 꼽히고, 자금을 회사 밖에 쓰겠다고 밝힌 표본의 반응이 더 좋음 
연구 핵심 발견 인용 수 
Johnson 외(2000, AER) 지배주주를 위해 회사의 자산과 이익을 옮기는 「터널링」 개념 정리 2,211 
Claessens 외(2002, JF) 동아시아 기업에서 지배권이 현금흐름권을 웃돌수록 기업가치 하락 3,842 
Bae·Kang·Kim(2002, JF) 한국 기업집단 계열사 인수에서 인수회사 주가는 하락, 지배주주는 다른 계열사 가치 상승으로 이득 1,170 
Baek·Kang·Lee(2006, JF) 한국 재벌 계열사의 사모 증권 발행에서 발행가를 통해 지배주주 쪽으로 가치 이전 576 
Schipper·Smith(1986, JFE) 자회사 공모 발표 때 모회사 주가 상승(같은 회사의 유상증자 발표와 반대) 362 
Allen·McConnell(1998, JF) 공모 자금을 주로 부채 상환·배당에 쓰겠다고 밝힌 표본 누적초과수익률 +6.63%, 투자 목적 보유 표본 −0.01% 174 
Lamont·Thaler(2003, JPE) 미국 기술주 자회사 상장에서 모회사 시가총액이 보유 자회사 지분가치보다 낮은 가격 오류가 공매도 제약으로 지속 660 
Doidge 외(2004, JFE) 미국에 교차상장한 외국 기업의 토빈 q 가 같은 나라 기업보다 16.5% 높음(1997년 표본) 1,627 
Baker·Wurgler(2006, JF) 투자 심리가 높았던 시기 뒤에는 가치평가·차익거래가 어려운 주식의 수익률이 상대적으로 낮음 6,011

주: 인용 수는 OpenAlex 기준(2026년 10월 14일 조회) 
자료: 각 논문, OpenAlex, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

4 
 II. 지정학: 다롄 공장과 미국 거점

1. 미·중 승인 아래 놓인 다롄, 미국 거점을 검토하는 솔리다임

낸드 생산 거점, 
중국 다롄 
인수 대금 88억달러 
 솔리다임 낸드는 인텔에서 넘겨받은 중국 랴오닝성 다롄 공장에서 만든다. SK하이닉스
는 인텔 낸드 사업 인수에 2021년 12월 66억달러, 2025년 3월 22억달러를 치렀다(SK
하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026). 다롄은 192단 제품을 만든다(Blocks & Files, 2026.9.18). 
장비 반입은 
해마다 허가 
미국 상무부 · 2025.8.29 
 미국 상무부는 2025년 8월 29일 장비 반입 포괄 허가(VEU) 명단에서 다롄 공장(옛 
Intel Semiconductor (Dalian))과 SK하이닉스 중국 법인을 빼기로 했다. 고시의 시행일
은 2025년 12월 31일이다(BIS, 2025)[그림4]. 대신 해마다 필요한 미국산 장비 계획을 
내고 승인받는 방식으로 바꿨고, 회사는 2025년 12월 2026년분 연간 허가를 받았다
(SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 생산은 이어지지만, 장비 반입은 해마다 미국의 허
가를 받아야 한다. 
중국 쪽 조건도 
12월 이후 해제 신청 
 중국 시장감독관리총국(SAMR)은 인텔 낸드 인수를 승인하며 2021년 12월부터 5년간 
기업용 SSD(PCIe·SATA)의 중국 판매가 상한, 생산 확대, 경쟁사 진입 지원 등을 조건
으로 붙였다. 판매가는 거래 조건이 같으면 승인 전 24개월 평균을 넘지 못한다
(SAMR, 2021). 회사는 이 조건 때문에 2026년 중국에 파는 낸드 제품 값을 크게 올리
기 어렵다고 밝혔다. 5년이 지나면 해제를 신청할 수 있고, 승인 여부는 당국이 정한다
(SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 다롄은 미국의 장비 반입 허가와 중국의 가격·생산 
조건, 두 가지 틀 안에서 운영된다.

[그림2] SK하이닉스 F-1/A 원문 → 장비 반입은 해마다 허가, 중국 판매가는 5 년 조건, 2025년 매출의 68.8%는 미국 판매법인

자료: SEC, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

ESG [한화리서치]

5 
 2. 미국 공장 검토와 남은 일정

미국 공장 검토, 
공모 자금이 재원 후보 
 솔리다임은 미국 동부(뉴욕주가 후보로 거론)에 낸드 공장을 짓는 방안을 검토하고 있
다(Reuters, 2026.9). 미국 공장에는 다롄에 들이기 어려운 최신 장비를 들일 수 있다. 
신주로 조달한 공모 자금은 그 건설 재원이 될 수 있다. 솔리다임 낸드 공장과는 별개
로, SK하이닉스는 미국 인디애나에 첨단 패키징 공장을 짓고 있다. 2028년 10월 클린룸
을 열고 2029년 하반기 양산할 계획이다(착공식 발표, 2026.8.27). 이 사업은 요건을 채
우면 미국 정부 보조금 최대 4.58억달러와 대출 최대 5.7억달러를 받을 수 있다(SK하이
닉스 F-1/A, 2026). 미·중 승인과 상장 일정은 [그림3]에 모았다.

[그림3] 솔리다임과 다롄 공장을 둘러싼 일정 → 다롄은 미·중 두 조건 아래, 미국 공장은 생산 거점 분산·상장은 자금 조달의 선택지

주: VEU 제외는 BIS(2025), 인수 대금·중국 조건·연간 허가는 SK하이닉스 F-1/A(2026), 나머지는 DART 공시와 언론 보도 기준. 당사 투자의견과 무관 
자료: SEC, DART, Bloomberg, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림4] 미국 연방관보 원문: VEU 명단 제외 → 다롄 공장(옛 인텔 다롄)이 이름으로 명시, 2025년 12월 31일 시행

주: 미국 연방관보 90 FR 42321(2025.9.2) 
자료: 미국 연방관보(BIS), 한화투자증권 리서치센터

6 
 III. 에너지 전환: 전력이 고르는 저장장치

1. 데이터센터 전력 제약과 대용량 SSD

전력이 모자라면 
용량당 전력이 관건 
 국제에너지기구(IEA)는 세계 데이터센터 전력 사용이 2024년 415TWh에서 2030년 
945TWh로 늘 것으로 본다[그림5]. 2024년 사용량의 45%가 미국이다. 전력이 제약이 
되면 같은 전력에 더 많이 담는 저장장치가 유리하다. 솔리다임은 122TB QLC(셀당 4
비트 낸드) SSD로 저장장치를 꾸리면 HDD·SSD 혼용 구성보다 설치 공간은 최대 4분
의 1, 저장장치 전력은 최대 84% 준다고 밝혔다(Solidigm, 2024). 
미국 공장은 공급망, 
상장은 자금 조달 
 NPS의 낸드 사업 반기 매출은 1년 새 3.7배가 됐다[그림9]. SK하이닉스 전체 매출의 
68.8%가 미국 판매법인에서 나왔다(2025년; SK하이닉스 F-1/A, 2026)[그림2]. 데이터
센터 전력 증가도 미국이 이끌었다[그림6]. 미국 공장은 공급망을, 미국 상장은 자금 조
달을 넓히는 수단이 될 수 있다.

[그림5] 세계 데이터센터 전력 사용 → 2030년 945TWh로 2024년의 2.3배, 2024년 사용량의 45%가 미국

자료: IEA, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림6] IEA 원문: 지역별 데이터센터 전력 사용 → 2017년 이후 증가는 미국이 주도

자료: IEA, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

7 
 IV. 지배구조: 상장 전 지분

1. 지분 사슬과 계열사 출자

계열사의 
NPS 출자 약정 
약정 합계 11.1억달러 
 솔리다임은 SK하이닉스의 미국 법인 NPS(SK hynix NAND Product Solutions)가 지분 
96.86%를 가진 회사다(6월 말). 올해 SK㈜(2.5억달러), SK이노베이션(미국 법인 경유 
3.8억달러), SK텔레콤(4.8억달러, 4년 분할)이 NPS 신주를 받기로 약정했다[표2]. NPS는 
외국법인이어서 국내 계열회사 주식 소유 제한(공정거래법 제18조)의 대상이 아니다. 
직접 지분의 가치는 
SK㈜ 몫 
 SK㈜는 SK스퀘어 지분 32.2%를, SK스퀘어는 SK하이닉스 지분 20.0%를, SK하이닉스
는 NPS 지분 98.16%를 갖고 있어 지분율을 곱한 SK㈜의 NPS 간접 지분은 약 6.3%
다. 여기에 직접 지분 1.27%가 더해졌다(6월 말). SK㈜는 별도·연결 재무제표 모두에서 
NPS를 관계기업으로 처리한다. 상장에 따른 시장가치 변화가 장부에 바로 잡히지는 않
지만, 직접 지분의 가치 변동은 지분을 가진 SK㈜에 귀속된다[그림7].

[그림7] SK그룹 지분도와 솔리다임 상장 사슬 → 지주회사·자회사·손자회사가 모두 상장된 사슬 아래, 계열사가 NPS에 직접 출자

주: 지분율은 2026년 6월 말 기준(SK스퀘어의 SK하이닉스 지분은 7월 ADR 신주 상장 뒤 주식 수 기준) 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표2] 공시 원문 발췌: 계열사의 NPS 출자 → 세 회사 약정 11.1억달러, SK텔레콤은 642주를 3,971억원에 취득하기로 결정 
공시 원문 발췌 
SK텔레콤(2026.6.29, 7.23 정정공시) 발행회사 SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.(미국). 취득주식수 642주, 취득금액 397,129,047,957원, 취득 후 지분 0.62%. 현금출자, 취득예정일 2026-07-31(7.23 정정) 
같은 공시: 취득목적 「당사 AI 사업과의 시너지 확보 측면에서, SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.의 지분 확보 추진」 
SK㈜(2026.8.14) 「총 약정금액은 USD 250,000천이며, 당반기말 현재 USD 133,891천의 출자가 완료되었습니다」. 같은 주석의 SK텔레콤 약정: 「총 약정금액은 USD 480,000천이며, … 2026년 6월 25일부터 4 년간」 
SK이노베이션(2026.8.14) 「2026년 7 월 23일자로 관계기업인 SK hynix NAND Product Solutions Corp.에 USD 143.4백만을 추가 출자하였습니다」. 약정 총액 USD 380,000천(SK Innovation America 경유)

자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

8 
 2. 출자 단가와 실적

기준이 다른 
세 가치 지표 
 SK㈜ 반기보고서의 NPS 장부가와 지분율로 역산한 장부가 기준 NPS 가치는 약 16조
원이다. SK텔레콤의 취득 단가(642주, 3,971억원)에 신주 발행 전 주식 수(101,875주)를 
곱하면 약 63조원이다. 장부가 역산과 거래 단가는 산출 기준이 달라, 두 값의 차이만으
로 출자 가격의 적정성을 판단할 수는 없다. 낸드 사업 반기 순이익은 지난해 상반기 
0.13조원에서 올해 상반기 5.84조원으로 늘었다[그림9]. 보도된 상장 기업가치(약 136
조원)는 NPS가 아니라 솔리다임 기준 값이다[그림8]. 
출자 단가는 
계열사 공시로 확인 
 출자 단가는 신주를 받은 계열사의 공시와 주석에서 확인할 수 있다[표2]. SK텔레콤 약
정 잔여분(4년 분할)과 외부 프리IPO가 어떤 단가와 규모로 이뤄지는지가 상장 전 SK
하이닉스 주주의 몫을 정할 것이다.

[그림8] 기준이 다른 세 가치 지표(NPS·솔리다임) → 평가 대상·산출 방식이 달라 직접 비교에는 한계

주: 5~6월은 SK㈜ 별도 장부가(1,997억원) ÷ 지분율(1.27%)로 역산값. 세 값은 산출 기준이 달라 직접 비교에 한계. 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림9] NPS 낸드 사업 반기 실적 → 매출 3.7배, 순이익 0.13조원에서 5.84조원으로

주: SK하이닉스 반기보고서 연결 주석의 NPS 요약재무 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

9 
 3. 솔리다임의 비중

기업가치로는 
시가총액의 약 13% 
10월 13일 종가 기준 
 NPS 낸드 사업이 SK하이닉스 연결에서 차지하는 몫은 상반기 매출의 9.3%, 순이익의 
4.3%, 자산의 5.7%, 순자산의 3.1%다. 순이익 비중이 낮은 것은 연결 순이익에 금융상
품 평가이익 등이 반영됐기 때문이다. 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 10월 13일 SK하
이닉스 시가총액(1,054조원)의 약 13%다(회계 비중과는 기준이 다름)[그림10]. 
회사 입장은 
확정된 사항 없음

SK하이닉스는 8월 5일과 9월 4일 보도 관련 공시에서 솔리다임이 경쟁력 강화를 위해 
다양한 방안을 검토하고 있으나 확정된 사항은 없다고 밝혔다. 다음 재공시 예정일은 
12월 3일이다. SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득 공시와 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 원문은 [그
림11]에 실었다.

[그림10] SK하이닉스 안의 NPS(낸드) 비중 → 회계 숫자로는 3~9%, 보도된 기업가치로는 시가총액의 약 13%

주: NPS 낸드 사업 ÷ SK하이닉스 연결(2026년 6월 말). 연결 순이익에는 금융상품 평가이익 등 금융수익(상반기 82.9조원)이 반영. 기업가치 136조원은 1,000억달러 × 1,360원(SBS 
Biz, 2026.10.8 환산) 
자료: DART, KRX, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[그림11] 공시 원문: SK텔레콤의 NPS 취득과 SK하이닉스의 보도 관련 공시 → 642주 3,971억원 취득 결정, 상장은 「확정된 사항 없음」

자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

10 
 4. 해외 상장에 적용되는 규율

8월부터 
엄격한 특례심사 
거래소 규정 · 8월 3일 
 금융위원회는 7월 31일 거래소 상장·공시 규정 개정을 의결해 8월 3일부터 중복상장을 
엄격한 특례심사 대상으로 바꿨다. 일반주주 권익을 고려하지 않는 비대칭적 중복상장
은 엄격히 금지한다는 취지다. 모회사 이사회는 독립 특별위원회 심의를 거쳐 주주영향
평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 찬반 결의, 공시라는 다섯 가지 의무를 진다. 
금융위원회는 이 의무를 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용한다고 밝혔다(금융위, 
2026.7.31)[그림12]. 
거래소 심사 미적용, 
당국은 규율 적용 입장 
 해외 상장은 거래소의 중복상장 특례심사를 거치지 않는다. 다만 금감원장은 금융위·금
감원 모두 중복상장에 해당한다고 본다고 답했다(10.19 국정감사). 주주동의가 필수인 
곳은 물적분할 자회사뿐이고, 솔리다임 같은 일반 자회사는 권고 대상이다(금융위, 
2026.7.31). 국회에는 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의를 받게 하는 자본시장법 개
정안(안도걸 의원 등 14인, 9월 9일 발의)이 정무위원회에 있다[표3].

[그림12] 금융위원회 중복상장 제도개선 구조도(원문) → 모회사 이사회 5 대 의무는 자회사 해외상장 때에도 적용

자료: 금융위원회, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

[표3] 솔리다임 상장에 적용되는 규율 → 거래소 심사는 미적용이나 당국은 규율 적용 입장, 이사회 의무가 핵심 
규율 내용 솔리다임 적용 
공정거래법 제18조 지주회사는 자회사 외, 자회사는 손자회사 외 국내 계열회사 주식 소유 금지. 손자회사는 국내 계열회사 주식을 100% 가질 때만 소유 대상 아님(외국법인) 
거래소 중복상장 특례심사 자회사의 독립성, 이사회 5 대 의무 이행과 찬성 결의, 주주동의 여부 심사 미적용(미국 상장) 
모회사 이사회 5 대 의무 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안, 주주 소통·동의 확인, 특별위원회 심의 뒤 찬반 결의, 단계별 공시 적용(금융위원회) 
주주동의 물적분할 자회사는 필수, 그 밖의 자회사는 권고. 3% 초과 의결권 제한, 출석 과반·전체 4분의 1 이상 권고(일반 자회사) 
자본시장법 개정안 자회사 상장 때 모회사 주총 특별결의(의안 2221240, 9월 9일 발의; SBS Biz, 2026.10.8) 정무위원회 계류

주: 중복상장 규정은 2026.7.31 의결, 8.3 시행. 해외 상장 적용은 금융위원회 보도참고자료(2026.7.31) 기준 
자료: 국가법령정보센터, 금융위원회, 국회 의안정보시스템, 언론 보도, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 11

ESG [한화리서치]

11 
 V. 주주가치: 공모 구조와 자금의 쓰임

1. 공모 구조별 SK 하이닉스 주주 손익

거래 직후 기준, 
신주든 구주든 가치 중립 
 공모가가 공정하면 신주 발행이든 구주매출이든 SK하이닉스 몫(지분가치와 현금의 합)
은 같다. 차이는 현금이 어디에 남느냐다. 신주를 내면 현금은 솔리다임에 남아 미국 공
장 등 투자 재원이 될 수 있고, 구주를 팔면 대금은 먼저 NPS에 들어오고, NPS의 배당
을 거쳐야 SK하이닉스의 환원 재원이 될 수 있다[표4]. 
전액 환원 가정 시 
주당 약 1만8,300원 
가정 · 100억달러 
 보도된 규모인 100억달러(약 13.6조원)를 구주매출로 조달하고, NPS가 이를 배당해 SK
하이닉스가 받은 현금(NPS 지분 98.16%분)을 다시 전액 특별배당한다고 가정하면 주
당 약 1만8,300원이다. 10월 13일 종가의 1.3%, 2025 사업연도 주당 배당(3,000원)의 6
배다. 다만 8월에 결정한 소각용 자사주 매입(40조원)과 견주면 3분의 1 규모다. 회사의 
배당 계획이 아니라 규모를 가늠하기 위한 가정 계산이다. 
추가 출자의 
가격과 조건 
 공모 전 외부 지분이 어떤 값과 조건으로 정해지는지도 SK하이닉스 주주의 몫을 좌우
한다. 출자 이후의 가치 상승분은 그 지분을 가진 계열사에 돌아간다. 다만 과거 출자 
단가가 상장 때의 기업가치보다 낮았다는 것만으로 기존 주주에게 불리한 거래였다고 
볼 수는 없다. 남은 출자와 프리IPO의 단가가 공정가에 가까울수록 SK하이닉스 주주의 
몫은 지켜진다. 
공모 구조의 평가, 
쓰임과 투자수익이 결정 
실적 개선 뒤의 값 
 보도된 기업가치(약 136조원)는 최근 실적 개선 뒤에 나온 값이다[그림8]. 이 값이 적정
한지는 이익이 이어지는지에 달려 있다. 구주매출은 판 지분만큼 지금의 평가를 현금으
로 바꾸는 길이고, 신주 발행은 지분을 일부 내주는 대신 미국 투자 재원을 솔리다임에 
넣어 수익으로 돌려받는 길이다. 신주로 조달한 자금을 미국 공장에 쓴다면, 주주가치는 
투자수익과 지정학적 위험을 줄이는 효과에 달려 있다. 어떤 공시가 나오면 무엇을 볼
지는 [표7]에 정리했다.

[표4] 공모 구조별 SK하이닉스 몫(가정) → 공정가면 지분가치와 현금의 합은 같고, 현금이 남는 곳만 다름 
구분 신주 발행 100% 구주매출 100% 
조달액(가정) 100억달러(약 13.6조원) 같음 
현금이 남는 곳 솔리다임(미국 공장 재원 가능) NPS·SK하이닉스(환원 재원 가능) 
SK하이닉스 실질 지분(6월 말 95.08%) 약 86.4% 약 85.3% 
SK하이닉스 몫(보도 기업가치 기준) 약 129조원 약 116조원 + 현금 13.3조원 
전액 환원 가정 시 주당 공모 현금은 솔리다임에 약 1만 8,300원(10/13 종가의 1.3%)

주: 1,000억달러를 공모 전 지분가치로 보고 100억달러를 조달한다고 둔 가정 계산. 거래 직후의 산술 비교로 주가 영향·투자수익·세금은 반영하지 않음. 당사 투자의견과 무관 
자료: DART, 블룸버그, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 12

ESG [한화리서치]

12 
 2. 종목별 영향과 판단 변수

종목마다 
받는 영향이 달라 
 솔리다임 상장이 SK그룹 상장사에 미치는 영향은 종목마다 다르다. SK하이닉스의 솔리
다임 실질 지분은 6월 말 공시 기준 95.08%로, 공모 구조·출자 가격·자금의 쓰임이 주
요 변수다. SK스퀘어는 SK하이닉스 지분을 통해 간접적으로 영향을 받는다. SK하이닉
스의 환원 정책과 보유 자산가치가 함께 반영된다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 직접 지분의 가치 변화와 남은 출자 단가를 함께 봐야 한다[표5]. 
상장은 낸드 사업의 
가격표 
 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이닉스 연결 안에 있던 낸드 사업에 시장 가격을 따로 붙인다. 시
가총액의 약 13%로 보도된 기업가치가 독립된 값으로 평가받는 계기다[그림10]. 회사
는 8월에 40조원 규모의 소각용 자사주 매입을 결정해 환원 원칙을 이미 보여 줬다. 시
장이 확인하려는 것은 구주매출로 현금이 생길 때 이 원칙이 이어지는지다. 변동성이 
큰 시기에는 주주보호 방안을 먼저 공개할수록 시장의 신뢰를 빨리 얻을 수 있다. 
판단을 바꿀 
변수 네 가지 
 이 판단을 바꿀 변수는 네 가지다. 상장 시점은 메모리 업황과 미국 기술주 공모 시장의 
분위기에 달려 있다. 미국의 2027년분 연간 허가는 다롄 장비 반입을 정한다. 중국 당
국의 조건 해제는 중국 판매가와 생산 의무를 바꾼다. 미국 공장은 투자 규모와 보조금 
조건이 확정돼야 수익성을 따질 수 있다. 
해외 상장, 
다른 그룹의 선례 
 해외 상장에는 거래소 심사가 적용되지 않지만, 당국은 중복상장 규율을 적용한다는 입
장이다. 금융위원회의 유권해석은 해외 자회사 상장 전반의 선례가 된다. 현대차(2024
년 10월)와 LG전자(2025년 10월)는 인도 법인을 현지에 상장하며 보유 주식을 파는 
구주매출을 택했다. 국회 법안이 대통령령으로 해외 증시까지 넓히면 모회사 주총 특별
결의가 해외 상장에도 걸린다(SBS Biz, 2026.10.8). 해외 자회사 상장을 검토하는 그룹은 
이 법안의 진행을 함께 봐야 한다.

[표5] SK그룹 상장사별 솔리다임 노출과 판단 조건 → 같은 상장이라도 종목마다 봐야 할 조건이 다름 
종목 지분 보유 경로 영향 경로 주요 판단 조건 
SK하이닉스 NPS 98.16% × 솔리다임 96.86%(실질 95.08%) 신주면 지분 희석·현금은 솔리다임에, 구주매출이면 NPS에 현금 유입. 주가는 메모리 업황이 주변수 공모가의 적정성, 신주·구주 비율, 조달 자금의 투자수익과 환원, 남은 출자 단가 
SK스퀘어 SK하이닉스 지분 20.0%를 거친 간접 노출 보유 자산가치와 SK하이닉스 환원 정책이 함께 반영 SK하이닉스의 환원 정책, 순자산가치 대비 할인율  
SK㈜ NPS 직접 1.27%(6월 말), SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스를 통한 간접 보유 직접 지분의 경제적 가치가 SK㈜에 귀속 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 2.5억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK텔레콤 NPS 0.62%(642주, 취득 결정 기준) 직접 지분의 가치 변화, 4년 분할 출자 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향, 총약정 4.8억달러 중 남은 납입액 
SK이노베이션 미국 법인 경유 NPS 출자(약정 3.8억달러) 미국 법인이 보유한 NPS 지분의 가치 변화와 추가 출자 부담 추가 출자의 단가·권리조건과 희석 영향,  잔여 약정의 납입 부담과 출자 재원

주: 지분은 2026년 6월 말 공시 기준(SK스퀘어는 7월 ADR 뒤, SK텔레콤은 취득 결정 기준). 노출 경로 정리로 투자의견·순위 아님 
자료: DART, 한화투자증권 리서치센터

### Page 13

ESG [한화리서치]

13 
 3. 확인할 공시와 결론

상장 사슬이 
네 단계로 
 SK㈜·SK스퀘어·SK하이닉스는 이미 모두 상장돼 있다. 솔리다임까지 상장하면 SK㈜에
서 솔리다임으로 이어지는 수직 사슬에 상장사가 네 곳 놓인다. 상장사가 늘어날수록 
각 단계의 주주에게 가치와 현금이 어떻게 전달되는지를 설명하는 일이 중요해진다. 
보호 방안의 
내용과 이행 
 가이드라인은 주주보호 방안의 예로 자회사 주식 현물배당과 자사주 소각을 든다. SK하
이닉스 이사회가 주주영향평가와 보호 방안을 언제, 어떤 내용으로 공시하는지가 확인
할 첫 문서다. 미국 공장의 필요성과 자회사 상장의 필요성은 따로 따질 문제다. 이사회
가 설명할 것은 다른 조달 수단과 견줘 상장이 기존 주주에게 유리한지, 그 근거는 무엇
인가다. 다음 재공시 예정일(12월 3일)을 포함해 확인할 일정은 [표6]에 정리했다. 
결론: 상장 기대보다 
조건 확인이 먼저

SK하이닉스 주가의 큰 방향은 여전히 메모리 업황이 정한다. 솔리다임 상장은 SK하이
닉스 안의 낸드 사업이 어떻게 평가받는지를 정하는 변수다. 그 평가를 가르는 조건은 
세 가지다. 상장 전 출자 단가가 적정한지, 공모 자금이 미국 투자와 모회사 가운데 어
디로 가는지, 투자수익이나 환원 계획이 구체적인지다. SK㈜·SK텔레콤·SK이노베이션은 
NPS 지분의 가치와 남은 출자 부담을 함께 봐야 한다. 상장과 미국 공장은 아직 확정
되지 않았다. 지금은 상장 기대를 미리 더하기보다, 금융위원회의 유권해석과 이사회의 
주주보호 방안, 12월 3일 재공시에서 이 조건을 확인할 때다.

[표6] 확인할 공시와 일정 → 공모가보다 상장 전 단가와 공모 자금의 쓰임이 먼저 
시점 확인할 것

12월 3 일 SK하이닉스 솔리다임 관련 보도 관련 공시의 재공시 예정일(9월 4 일 공시) 
12월 중국 인수 조건 5 년 경과: 해제 신청 여부와 당국 결정. 미국 2027년분 장비 반입 허가 여부(2026년분은 지난해 12월 발급) 
수시 SK하이닉스 이사회 특별위원회 구성, 주주영향평가, 주주보호 방안(자회사 주식 현물배당·자사주 소각 등) 공시 
수시 NPS 추가 신주 발행 단가: SK텔레콤 약정 잔여분(4년 분할), 외부 프리IPO 투자(보도)

주: 예정 일정은 바뀔 수 있음

[표7] 공시별 판단 기준 → 상장 여부보다 어떤 공시가 나오느냐가 판단을 바꿈 
공시로 확인되는 것 주주가치 판단의 초점 함께 볼 증거 
신주 중심 공모 지분율 하락보다 조달 자금의 투자수익 투자 규모·집행 일정·보조금 조건 
구주매출, 대금이 NPS에 남음 매각 가격과 현금의 재투자 계획 자금 사용 계획·세금·비용 
구주매출, 모회사 환원 구체화 SK하이닉스로 오는 현금과 환원 규모 배당·소각 결의와 집행 일정 
계열사 추가 출자·프리IPO 거래 당시의 가격·규모와 조건 외부 투자자 가격·주식 권리 
상장 지연·철회 기존 낸드 사업가치의 지속성 실적·현금흐름·대체 자금 조달

주: 판단 기준의 정리로 전망·투자의견 아님

저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 이 자료는 특정 종목의 매수·매도를 권유하지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터
가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 
책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• Recent revisions to US PCE inflation, UK productivity
and Canadian population estimates are reminders that
statistical measurement is imperfect. Revisions are both a
fact of life and, when they improve measurement, desirable.
• Australian data will also be revised over time, though

not necessarily in the same ways as these latest revisions
abroad. The ABS tends to favour quality over speed,
which reduces the need for early revisions as more data
become available. But revisions can still matter. Population
estimates implied in the initial releases of labour market and
GDP data are especially likely to be revised in the near term.
• Estimates of current trends can change as future data are
published. Shifting seasonal patterns are another source

of revision, especially for monthly CPI components with
short histories. Series that are derived from multiple other
imperfectly measured series are more prone to revision
than quantities that can be measured directly. All these
considerations point to a need for humility when using
recent data to make strong policy pronouncements.
On September 30, the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation,
PCE, was revised down. New methods and data sources were
applied to three components of services inflation (financial

advice, software and legal services), large enough to result in
downward revisions to the overall read on core PCE inflation.
These changes had been pre-announced in June and were
therefore not a surprise. The CPI measure of inflation was not
affected, nor should the revisions materially change your view
of US inflationary pressures.
Some revisions are more consequential for interpretation and
policy. A couple of weeks before the US PCE revision, it was

revealed that the UK Office for National Statistics was revising
down its estimates of average hours worked per worker. The
result was that measured productivity growth was nowhere
near as weak as had been widely believed. As my friend and
counterpart, TS Lombard’s Chief Economist Freya Beamish
commented on social media, “Oh well. Not like any major policy
decisions were taken on the basis of those numbers.” Quite.
Also in September, Statistics Canada revised up its estimates

of Canada’s population, taking the recent growth rates
from outright contraction to still-slow growth a little below
½%yr. The number of temporary residents in Canada is still
falling, but the revised data do a better job of tracking them,
especially those seeking an extension on their permit. (An alert
When reality gets revised
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
for customers who have seen the cross-country population
graph I often use at client events: the Canadian line now looks

different but still shows a sharp slowing.)
There is no fault to be attributed here. Revisions to key
statistics are a part of economic life. Sometimes the underlying
data arrive with a lag, perhaps because of reporting deadlines.
Sometimes the compiling agency improves its methods or
gains access to a better data source. The US and UK examples
above fall into this category. In the Canadian case, better
data and a more accurate definition of scope were involved.

All these revisions should be welcomed. They provide a more
accurate picture of the underlying reality.
Australia, revised
Revisions are a fact of life for Australian statistics, too. I am
still a little scarred by that unexpected –0.5%qtr GDP print
when the September quarter 2016 data were first released on
my first day as RBA Assistant Governor; the figure has since
been revised to 0%qtr and was followed by a big bounce-back
in the December quarter.

The recent revisions mentioned above are less likely to be
replicated in Australia, though. Australia’s CPI is designed such
that the headline (not seasonally adjusted) measure is never
revised. This is because it is used in many legal contracts.
If an error is detected, the ABS makes the adjustment in
subsequent months and quarters rather than revising the
history. Our labour force survey has a much higher response
rate than its UK equivalent – Australians are a compliant lot

when the ABS comes knocking – and measures actual hours
worked more reliably. And our population statistics already
handle the array of temporary resident visas with the Bureau
instead applying a standard 12-month-in-16-month residency
test to count as a resident for the population statistics rather
than using a visa-classification basis.
This last case illustrates Australia’s broader tendency to
favour quality over speed in official statistics. Our CPI and

national accounts data tend to be published a bit later than
peer countries’ data for the same reference period, and our
“Economic data are often revised. Sometimes
the revisions change the story; sometimes
they do not."

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
estimated resident population data is quite a bit lagged
relative to Canada’s (the 12-month-in-16 criteria taking over
a year to confirm). The trade-off is that observers of the
Australian economy must wait longer but can be less worried
about “flash” and “advance” releases of data that can be

quickly revised to tell a completely different story.
Revisions to data still can matter in Australia, and it helps
to be aware of the specific cases where it might occur. For
example, precisely because the population data are very
lagged, the population estimates underlying the initial
releases of the labour force survey and national accounts are
based on preliminary data and get revised later as a matter
of routine. This is why ratios such as the unemployment,

employment-to-population and participation rates provide a
better real-time gauge of labour market conditions than the
monthly change in employment. Similarly, GDP per capita can
be revised even when GDP is not. This type of revision is also
a more likely source of short-term changes in measured hours
worked in the Australian productivity data than the conceptual
change that shifted the UK data.
The usual reporting lags – and occasional correction of

reporting errors by the reporting entity – will always be a factor
in Australia just as it is in other countries. The annual national
accounts, due on 23 October, incorporate additional data, revise
the quarterly estimates and provide first estimates of important
quantities such as the capital stock. Just as importantly, though,
the annual accounts reconcile the different ways of measuring
GDP. In doing so, what we thought we knew about the recent

history of output growth can change.
Every purchase is also a sale, so conceptually, you should get the
same answer for final output regardless of whether you count
how much is produced, how much people spent on it, or how
much income people got for producing it. In practice, though,
the “production”, “expenditure” and “income” approaches do
not always line up until the ABS goes through a more thorough
reconciliation process in compiling the annual accounts.

When the future changes the past
Future data can also change our view of the past. Two good
examples are trend estimation and seasonal adjustment. Future
observations help reveal whether an apparent trend is stable.
As a result, real-time trend estimates can differ considerably
from those calculated with the benefit of later data.
Identifying stable seasonal patterns also requires a reasonable
run of data. Future observations affect whether an earlier

movement is judged to be normal seasonal variation. This
problem is more pronounced for newer series, where there is
not yet enough history to establish what “normal” looks like.
Consider, then, the monthly CPI. This relatively new measure has
only a short run of data with many individual components having
too little history to use standard seasonal adjustment methods.
As Westpac’s senior economist Justin Smirk has previously
highlighted, some components such as footwear are being

‘seasonally adjusted’ by applying Henderson trends, similar
to those used in the ‘trend’ measures of labour market and
national accounts data. For some other components, including
books, furniture, audio-visual and computing equipment and
children’s clothes, certain price falls – usually associated with
sales periods – are just arbitrarily removed from the seasonally
adjusted series. The index is held at its previous month’s level
but otherwise matches the non-seasonally adjusted series.

The seasonally adjusted measures of these CPI components will
inevitably be revised over time, and thus so could the monthly
trimmed mean estimates. This helps explain why the RBA has
said it would, for now, focus on the quarterly trimmed mean
based on the old seasonal factors. That measure will at least
not be buffeted by shifting measurement of seasonality in a
short history. If seasonal patterns are themselves also shifting,
the current underlying trend might look a bit different in a few

years, not just a smoother month-to-month profile.
The broader point here is to remember that economic statistics
are a lens on reality. Sometimes the lens is smudged, sometimes
the scope is off and sometimes what is being read as underlying
trends is wrong. Measures constructed from several imperfect
series – productivity from output and hours worked, or unit labour
costs from productivity and labour costs, or savings rates derived
from income and spending – are especially prone to revision

compared with concepts that can be measured more directly, like
a price. That is a strong argument for humility before making firm
policy pronouncements based on simple data comparisons.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: mulling the minutes
consistent with a labour market that is gradually normalising
than one entering a meaningful downturn.
The September FOMC minutes reinforced a distinctly hawkish
message, with participants noting they had "not seen

sufficient progress on lowering inflation in recent months" and
expressing concern that inflation risks remained "skewed to the
upside". Policymakers highlighted that both core services and
core goods inflation remained elevated, with some attributing
persistent goods price strength to the effects of the AI buildout
despite the sector's traditional disinflationary role. There was
also concern that, after more than five years of inflation above
target, elevated inflation could begin to influence inflation

expectations and wage- and price-setting behaviour. In
contrast, labour market conditions were generally viewed as
stable and "close to maximum employment". While a majority
noted the labour market had strengthened somewhat recently,
several participants highlighted unusually low dynamism,
characterised by low hiring, low layoffs, a low job-finding
rate and elevated long-term unemployment. Importantly,
participants generally judged that aggregate wage growth

was moderate and "consistent with inflation moving toward 2
percent", with several explicitly noting that "the labor market
was not currently a source of inflationary pressures".
While participants broadly expected AI-related investment to
boost productivity growth and potential output over time, several
warned that "the AI buildout could cause aggregate demand
to outpace aggregate supply over the medium term, putting
upward pressure on inflation". Consistent with this view, some

participants had raised their estimate of the neutral policy rate
and several judged the current policy stance to be "not restrictive
or only mildly restrictive". Against this backdrop, all participants
supported the September rate increase, and most assessed
that another increase in the federal funds rate would likely be
appropriate by year-end, although future decisions would remain
data dependent.
The September ISM Services PMI released this week painted

a similar picture of an economy that remains resilient but is
gradually losing momentum. The headline index eased to 54.9
from 55.4 in August, with the decline driven by a sharp fall in
business activity and a 9.4pt drop in new export orders, which
slipped into contractionary territory for the first time in eight
The only data release of note in Australia this week was
the October Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Survey ,
which reported a 4.7% drop in the headline index. At

80.4, sentiment has tumbled back to deeply pessimistic
levels, similar to the post-pandemic cost-of-living shock
when readings were among the weakest in the survey’s
more than fifty-year history. Indeed, inflation and interest
rate pressures are once again clearly front of mind for
consumers, with both the view on ‘family finances vs a
year ago’ and ‘family finance next 12 months’ falling 8.0%
and 6.4% in the month, respectively. Moreover, the RBA’s

September rate hike looks to have had a significant impact –
sentiment tanked 20% between those surveyed before the
RBA decision and those surveyed afterwards.
Views on the economic outlook were relatively more resilient,
but they still weakened, with the one-year and five-year ahead
sub-indexes falling 1.6% and 0.4% respectively. Consumers
are becoming more ‘on edge’ about job security, albeit not
outright alarmed. The unemployment rate remains relatively

low versus history, although it has drifted up to a cycle high
of 4.6% as labour supply continues to outstrip demand. We
expect it to hold there in next week’s September Labour Force
Survey – see our preview for more detail. All told, with higher
interest rates, renewed inflation pressures and a softening
jobs market all weighing at once, households are entering the
final months of the year on the back foot.
Offshore, the focus was on US data.
Before turning to this week's releases, it is worth noting that

September non-farm payrolls came in softer than expected
across most metrics. Employment rose by only 29k (89k
expected), and there were sizeable downward revisions
to prior months, with a combined 60k removed from July
and August payrolls, leaving gains of just 31k and 29k
respectively. The details pointed to weaker hiring in leisure
& hospitality and education & health services industries,
although construction and manufacturing employment
continued to benefit from ongoing data-centre-related

investment. The unemployment rate edged up to 4.2%, with
softer employment occurring alongside a rise in labour force
participation to 61.8%. Meanwhile wage growth moderated
to 0.1%mth (3.0%yr). Taken together, the report suggests
labour demand is cooling, with the three-month average
pace of job creation slowing to around 50k. However, softer
payroll gains are not necessarily indicative of labour market
deterioration. Lower immigration and ageing demographics

have reduced the pace of employment growth needed to keep
the unemployment rate stable. As such, the report is more
Sian Fenner, Head of Business and Industry Economics
Ryan Wells, Economist

AI investment, productive capacity and
inflation."

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Fuel, housing and the bumpy road ahead
Higher fuel costs, a weaker exchange rate, and a softer
housing market have prompted a refresh of our near-term
forecasts. Inflation looks set to finish 2026 above 4%, while
growth could lose a little momentum into year-end. Even so,

we continue to expect the RBNZ to pause in October before
lifting the OCR to 3% in December.
New Zealand’s recovery is still moving forward, but the road
has become rougher. Since our last forecast update in August,
global oil and refined-fuel prices have climbed sharply, the
New Zealand dollar has fallen, and longer-term interest rates
have moved higher. At home, the housing market has remained
weaker than we anticipated. None of those developments,

on their own, are enough to derail the recovery. Together,
however, they point to a tougher finish to 2026 for households
and businesses. We have therefore revisited our near-term
inflation, housing and growth forecasts. The broad story
remains one of continued economic expansion and gradually
improving labour demand, but with a more pronounced
inflation spike and slightly less momentum in consumer
spending. Our OCR call is unchanged: a pause at the October

review, followed by a 25 basis point increase to 3% at the
December Monetary Policy Statement.
A temporary inflation reprieve
We continue to think that consumer prices rose 0.7% in the
September quarter. That would lower annual inflation from
4.1% in June to 3.7% in September, broadly consistent with
the monthly price updates published so far. The final monthly
release for the quarter is due on 16 October and will help firm up
that estimate. But any easing in the annual rate is likely to prove

short-lived. We now expect prices to rise 1.1% in the December
quarter, lifting annual inflation to a three-year high of 4.2% by
year-end. That is half a percentage point above our previous
projection and above the RBNZ’s September forecast of 3.9%.
The reason is easy to see at the petrol pump. Higher global
fuel prices have been amplified by a more than 4% fall in
the New Zealand dollar since the start of September. Local
petrol prices have risen by around 50 cents a litre over the

past month, with the nationwide average price of 91 unleaded
reaching a record $3.55 a litre this week. RBNZ Governor
Breman acknowledged a couple of weeks ago that higher
oil prices have tilted the risks around the near-term inflation
outlook to the upside. Importantly, however, the RBNZ remains
focused on the medium-term inflation outlook rather than
trying to offset every movement in volatile fuel prices.
For now, we have left our quarterly inflation forecasts for 2027

unchanged. Even so, the higher starting point at the end of this
year means annual inflation is likely to remain firm through much
of 2027. Our updated profile has inflation at 3.7% in March
2027, 2.6% in June, 2.5% in September and 1.7% in December.
That is broadly similar to the RBNZ’s path through the first three
quarters, although our forecast falls more quickly late in the
year. We will take a fuller look at the 2027 inflation and growth
outlook next week alongside the monthly Market Outlook.

Housing fails to find its feet
The housing market has also delivered a disappointment. We
had expected prices to stabilise through the second half of
2026, but sales volumes have continued to weaken, and the
median time required to sell a property has lengthened to 48
days. Average prices have kept edging lower, despite firmer
conditions in parts of the South Island with greater exposure
to the strong export economy. The latest indicators do not
suggest that a decisive turn is close. We have therefore revised

our forecast for national house price growth in 2026 from a
0.6% rise to a 1.4% decline. That incorporates quarterly falls
of 0.5% in September and 0.8% in December.
The headwinds are familiar: the labour market remains soft,
mortgage rates are higher and employment growth is still
moderate. Those forces are likely to keep the market subdued
for some time, with risks tilted towards another weak year
in 2027. Changes to the taxation of capital gains on investor

housing following the 2026 General Election may add another
potential drag.
Kelly Eckhold
Chief Economist NZ

### Page 7

7
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Regional outcomes will continue to differ. Tourism- and
agriculture-focused areas should benefit from stronger income
and employment growth, supported by robust commodity export
prices. We have lifted our 2026/27 Fonterra Milk Price forecast to
$9.75/kg and set an opening forecast of $9.50/kg for 2027/28. In

contrast, homeowners in the major North Island cities may have to
wait longer for the wealth boost that comes with rising property
values. Data released this week shows that household wealth
continues to be dragged down by declining housing values.
A little less momentum
The weaker housing backdrop matters for the wider economy.
When petrol absorbs more of the household budget and home
values are drifting lower, consumers tend to become more
cautious. That combination is likely to crimp discretionary

spending in the run-up to Christmas and could take some
steam out of the construction recovery. That’s even though our
own card spending data for September  showed resilience in
consumer spending. Recent business and consumer confidence
surveys have nevertheless remained resilient despite the rise
in energy prices and long-term interest rates. Our short-term
growth forecasts therefore remain broadly intact, but we have
trimmed our December quarter consumer spending projection

and now expect GDP growth of 2.5% over 2026. That is still
a solid result, although the balance of risks has shifted to
the downside.
There are several moving parts to watch. Sustained high fuel
prices would squeeze household purchasing power more than
we have allowed for. The sharp lift in long-term interest rates
may also weigh on activity through 2027, even if shorter-
term borrowing costs evolve as expected. The election result
introduces two-sided risks that are difficult to quantify before

the shape and timing of policy changes are known. Against that,
strong agricultural incomes and signs of improving employment
growth should provide a buffer. We have not changed our 2026
labour market forecasts. Hiring appears to be strengthening
gradually, but it will take time before that improvement
translates into a noticeable fall in the unemployment rate.
The RBNZ should still pause before Christmas
Despite the higher near-term inflation track, we are not

changing our OCR forecast. Since the July Monetary Policy
Review, our view has been that the RBNZ would slow the pace
of increases as the OCR approached the Bank’s estimate of a
neutral 3%. The September meeting clearly signalled a pause
in October and one further rise in December. We continue to
regard that as the most likely path. The latest developments
raise headline inflation but, so far, there is little evidence that
inflation expectations are moving higher or that the fuel shock

is generating broader, persistent price pressure. At the same
time, growth risks warrant some caution.
A pause in October would give the Monetary Policy Committee
time to assess the cumulative effect of the increases already
delivered and to refine its strategy for 2027. Financial
conditions have also tightened from an unexpected source:
longer-term interest rates have risen markedly over the past
six weeks. That increase has at least offset the easing impulse

from the lower exchange rate, even though the weaker
currency is adding to imported inflation in the near term. We
still think the RBNZ is likely to have more work to do in 2027,
but there is plenty of data to come before that judgement
needs to be made.
For now, the best description of the outlook is resilient but
less comfortable. Export incomes remain strong, activity is
expanding and the labour market is beginning to improve. But
households face an awkward combination of record petrol

prices, soft home values and higher borrowing costs. That
mix points to a bumpy end to the year: inflation will be hotter,
spending growth a little cooler and the recovery more uneven
across regions. The RBNZ can afford to wait in October, but if
the economy develops broadly as we expect, a further hike to
3% in December will still be needed.

Looking through the monthly swings, while momentum has

current pace of working age population growth.
Our preview provides a deeper look into the various gauges

is sluggish but broader measures in the Q2 Labour Account

AUS: Sep Labour Force – Unemployment Rate (%)
Oct 15, Last: 4.6, Westpac f/c: 4.6 
Market f/c: 4.6, Range: 4.5 to 4.7
The participation rate jumped to 67.1% in August, just under 
the 115-year high recorded in January 2025. With labour 
supply once again outstripping demand, the unemployment 
rate rose from 4.5% to 4.6% – the highest since in late-2021, 
close to rounding up to 4.7% (4.646% at three decimal places).
A softer economy and sub-par employment growth typically 
discourages some people to search for work, but cost-of-living 
pressures and interest rate rises are more than outweighing this 
effect, encouraging more people into the labour market.
With the participation rate expected to pare back to 67.0%, we 
think the unemployment rate will hold steady at 4.6%. Please 
see our preview for more detail.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
NZ: Sep REINZ House Sales and Prices (%ann)
Oct 14, Sales Last: –13.0, Prices Last: –0.9
The housing market has remained soft in recent months,
with sales falling to a two-year low and prices edging lower.
Mortgage rates have risen as the RBNZ has initiated a new

tightening cycle, and a growing supply of unsold homes on the
market means that buyers are under no pressure to bid prices
higher. Conditions remain stronger in the south, with prices
up on last year in Canterbury, Otago and Southland, while
Auckland and Wellington are the weakest markets.
NZ: Sep Retail Card Spending (%mth)
Oct 16, Last: –0.9, Westpac f/c: 0.8
We’re forecasting a 0.8% rise in retail spending in September,
reversing the 0.9% fall seen in the previous month. In part, the

forecast rise in spending is due to the sharp increases in petrol
prices in recent weeks. Those higher fuel costs are weighing on
spending in other categories like hospitality. Nevertheless, we
still expect a 0.5% rise in core (ex-fuel) spending. Stats NZ’s
monthly retail spending figures have been very choppy for the
past two years, and we’re likely to see a bounce following last
month’s unexpected falls in categories like furnishings.
NZ: Sep Selected Consumer Prices (%mth)

Oct 16
This will be the last monthly update ahead of the full quarterly
CPI release on 22 October. We’re currently forecasting a 0.7%
quarterly rise in the CPI (+3.7%yr) and will be watching the
detail of the Selected Prices update to pin down that forecast.
In terms of the big categories, we’re forecasting a roughly 6%
rise in petrol costs, along with a 0.7% rise in food prices. That’s
balanced against continued softness in housing rents (which
are the single largest component of the CPI). The key sources

of risk for our forecast are the volatile travel categories, most
of which recorded sizeable drops last month that we expect to
partially reverse in September.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

6 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

or sale of any security or investment strategy. Merrill offers a broad range of brokerage, investment advisory and other services. There are important differences between brokerage and
investment advisory services, including the type of advice and assistance provided, the fees charged, and the rights and obligations of the parties. It is important to understand the differences,
particularly when determining which service or services to select. For more information about these services and their differences, speak with your Merrill financial advisor.

investment strategy and market views encompassing markets, economic indicators, asset classes and other market-related projections affecting GWIM.

of Bank of America Corporation.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 285: Chartpack _ Advanced economies_ Resilience to shock after shock (2026-10-06)

### Page 1

By Advanced Economies Team
Advanced Economies:
Resilience to shock after shock
October 2026

### Page 2

• The global economy is in the midst of a growth cycle. As a result, GDP growth is more resilient to shocks than initially
anticipated.
• The figures for the second quarter bear this out, despite the sharp rise in oil prices, whose negative impact on growth is offset
by a series of reflationary forces.
• The driving force behind the current growth cycle is clear: artificial intelligence (AI).
• The world’s major economies are currently engaged in a broader push to expand their capital goods investment.

• While European and US households are experiencing more or less the same energy price shock, inflation and household
consumption remain higher in the US.
• Higher and above-target inflation calls for monetary tightening.
• Inflation is exceeding the target set by three of the four central banks examined in this publication (the US, the UK, the
Eurozone). Inflation is currently expected to peak in Q4 2026 or Q1 2027, but there is a significant risk that energy inflation will

spread further to other components of inflation in the future.
• Inflation can also be a sign of (too much) economic strength (in the US).
• Monetary policies also have to offset the inflationary effects of fiscal policy (particularly in Japan).
• The outbreak of the conflict in Iran acted as a catalyst: the ensuing rise in energy prices contributes to the recent increase in
long-term rates (which had been trending downwards at the start of the year) to levels close to those of 2002.

• The other issue is the high level of fiscal deficits (too high for a period of growth), reflecting different fiscal trajectories. In the
US, the UK, France and Germany the public debt-to-GDP ratio is increasing, while it has stabilized in Italy and decreased in
Spain and Japan.
• The rise in long-term interest rates will weigh on public finances. The fiscal arithmetic is simpler today than it will be in 2030.
By 2030, the effective rate will have risen and caught up with GDP growth.

For more information, please refer to our publication:
EcoPerspectives – Advanced Economies | 3rd quarter of 2026, as of September 24, 2026– Economic Research– BNP Paribas
Advanced economies: Resilience to shock after shock

## Document 287: Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ getting the balance right (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
10:46 October 16 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September Labour Force Survey. Employment surprised
to the downside, -39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force participation from August’s near-
historic high largely offset. The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August was revised up.

Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless, the take home point from the update is that the

### Page 2

labour market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs below working-age population growth.
We expect the unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter financial conditions and cost-of-
living pressures restrict activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast peak is near at
4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the communication challenge for the RBA. The
September minutes were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing on materialising

upside risks to inflation. But, with the unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption of
4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the labour market is becoming harder to defend.
Still, with a clear focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our near-term CPI forecasts in
the October Market Outlook – we still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in September, taking the annual rate up to

3.7%yr. Energy prices were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation was a touch above
consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr). Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months (0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4%
(3.1%yr) on a 0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport services and a 0.3%
increase in medical care services (2.7%yr and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release of

the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures, with the headline and core measures up
6.3%yr and 5.0%yr respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these developments, firms
across multiple districts citing tariffs, insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost
pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect further price increases as contracts/hedges
roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is enough signal in these reports to warrant a

follow-up hike at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee members believe they are
near the end of the tightening cycle and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman both
showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear bias towards an October or December decision.
Williams stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in this cycle given known information.
And Waller also showed confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are doing some of the

FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating
that one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation near the 2.0%yr medium-term target.
However, as discussed in this month’s Market Outlook, capacity pressures are likely to see inflation
consistently above target, rather than below, in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity
in the housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to remain modestly restrictive over

the forecast horizon and will also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade account. At USD133.5bn, the September
result was a whisker inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More impressively, throughout
2025 and 2026 year-to-date the surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the average of
2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth of export growth is as impressive as the headline

annual gain of 36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese investment in
neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time, both trade and income receipts from offshore
investment should buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound foreign investment should
moderate the pace of gains against other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.

### Page 3

Related articles
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the evolution of producer prices across
Asia, and the likelihood of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent decades
continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was one of the key drivers of declining goods prices.
While still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing development to instead be driven by
capital, technology and energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking production are

also increasingly prioritising resilience, security and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore
more probably future supply-chain developments will help contain rising costs through productivity than
drive sustained price declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.

## Document 340: How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales

Source PDF: all report pdf/How AI agents could reshape retail as stores move beyond sales.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-08
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Agentic commerce, where autonomous AI agents
independently select and purchase products on behalf of
consumers, is forcing retailers to rethink their role. As AI takes
over more of the buying process, physical stores are evolving
from a sales channel into a destination for advice, service,
experience, and omnichannel support
Katinka Jongkind
A Galaxy General AI robot retail store, Shanghai. Retail is entering a new phase
of digital disruption, where agentic commerce could fundamentally change

how consumers shop
Agentic commerce fundamentally changes
the buying process
Retail is entering a new phase of digital disruption. E-commerce
has already transformed where consumers shop; agentic
commerce is set to change how they shop. Autonomous artiﬁcial
intelligence agents can search, compare and purchase products on
behalf of consumers, shifting shopping from an active task to an
increasingly automated background process.
